# The age profile of intergenerational persistence in the NLSY97, by race (with PSID / NLSY79 comparison)

Generated by `29.NLSY97AgeProfile.py` (2026-10-17). Every cell: coefficient (s.e.; p-value), *** p<0.01, ** p<0.05, * p<0.1; s.e. clustered by 1997 household (PSID: 1968 family; NLSY79: 1979 household), joint across bands (stacked, fully interacted), normal p-values; E[rank] p-values against 50. NLSY97 'White' = non-Black non-Hispanic. Black / White log IGE unweighted (as `26.IGEByAge.py`); Pooled = all races weighted by the round's cross-sectional weight; Pooled B+W = Black + White only, weighted; ranks always weighted (1997 weight). White - Black = difference of the two race regressions. N row: child-years (or child-bands) / children / clusters for Black (B) and White (W). Age = age at interview; income = previous calendar year. Parental income (main): 1996 gross household income (1997 parent interview), one year.

## 0. Data and samples

Respondents living with a parent figure in 1997 (born 1980-84). **Correction to the existing extract:** `3.NLSY97.py` codes CV_INCOME_GROSS_YR = -4 (valid skip) as income 0; in 1998-2000 the variable is valid-skipped for most youths at home, so `nlsy97_kids.pkl`'s `pinc_av` averages false zeros into the 1996 value (its mean is $27k vs $50k for `pinc_pt`) and `pinc_pt` carries false zeros. This script treats -4 as missing and saves its own extract (`data/nlsy97_ap_kids.pkl`, `data/nlsy97_ap_person_year.pkl`). The NLSY97 columns (2)-(3) of `ige_table.md` (parental mean at 12-17) use the contaminated `pinc_av`.

Parental income coverage (respondents at home in 1997):

## 4. Life-cycle mechanism (NLSY97)

### 4.1 Haider-Solon: growth by parental income

Children observed in both bands. One regression per column: (1) mean ln y in the early band, (2) in the late band, (3) late - early (controls: A quartic, missing-A; (3) also the change in mean age). (3) > 0: children of richer parents have steeper growth; (3) = the change of the IGE between the two ages in a fixed sample.

### 4.2 Hours vs wages: decomposition of the earnings growth gradient

Common child-year sample (earnings $516-$773k, annual hours >= 100, hourly wage $2-$200, 2000 dollars): band means of ln earnings, ln annual hours (CVC_HOURS_WK_YR_ALL, all jobs, income year) and ln hourly wage (earnings / hours); ln earnings = ln hours + ln wage row by row, so column (1) = (2) + (3) exactly. Growth gradient (late - early on ln parental income) and, in the second block, the late-band level IGE.

## 5. Reliability of the parental measure

### 5.1 One-year (1996) vs multi-year (1996-99 at home) parental income, same children

Children with the 1996 value and >= 1 further year at home (n_av >= 2; mostly the older 1980-82 cohorts, whose later years are youth-reported household income). One stacked regression per outcome x sex: column (1) one-year, (2) multi-year, (3) difference (2) - (1) with its joint s.e. Child-year design.

### 5.2 Reliability-corrected profile (IGE / RR, RR treated as known)

Main NLSY97 child-year estimates (unbalanced, one-year parental income) divided by a reliability ratio: (1) 0.57 for every group (PSID one-year family income); (2) PSID race-specific one-year ratios; (3) NLSY97 child-generation race-specific one-year ratios (both from `4.Estimates.py`, `ige_estimates.csv`). S.e. ignore the sampling error of RR. A common RR rescales the whole profile; race-specific ratios also move the gap.

RR used: common 0.57 (PSID one-year): B 0.570, W 0.570, pooled 0.570; PSID race-specific (4.Estimates): B 0.565, W 0.585, pooled 0.626; NLSY97 race-specific (4.Estimates): B 0.492, W 0.430, pooled 0.495

## 6. Comparison with the PSID and NLSY79 (same bands, same definitions)

PSID and NLSY79 frames of `26.IGEByAge.py` re-estimated on the four bands 25-29 ... 40-44 only (so the tests use the same bands). One regression per column and cell; columns = data sets. PSID parents: mean family income at child ages 13-19 (>= 3 years); NLSY79: 1978-80 mean; NLSY97: 1996 (one year) — the NLSY97 levels are attenuated relative to the PSID by the shorter parental average (column 4 rescales the NLSY97 estimate by 1 / 0.57 as a rough bound; the PSID multi-year measure itself has RR ~0.63-0.65). Pooled: PSID all races weighted, NLSY79 Black + non-Black non-Hispanic weighted (wt79), NLSY97 all races weighted.
